# Task
You will be given a **Requirement** and a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML. Your task is to annotate every operation listed in **Operations** with a specification.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Take the principle of modularization into account.
2. For each operation, determine its functional boundary based on the given requirement. Ensure high cohesion and low coupling.
3. If there is any unspecified details, such as undefined constants, rates, and ratios, then you must infer them.
4. Make sure that all requirements can be covered.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

```plantuml

title nexus Model

class EntanglementManager {
    activePairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    quantumComm : QuantumComm

    listActivePairs() : void

    removePair(pairIndex : int) : void
    sendMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : void
}
class JsonHelper {
    escapeString(s : String) : String
    parseArray(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<EJavaObject>
    parseBool(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : boolean
    parseNull(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    parseNum(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : Number
    parseObj(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EMap
    parseObject(json : String) : EMap
    parseStr(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : List<String>
    parseValue(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : EJavaObject
    serializeMap(map : EMap) : String
    skipWhitespace(s : String, pos : IntArrary) : void
    toJsonString(s : String) : String
    toJsonValue(value : EJavaObject) : String
}
class MessageProtocol {
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    addMetadata(key : String, messageDict : EMap, value : EJavaObject) : void
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap) : String
    getMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : EJavaObject
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    removeMetadata(messageDict : EMap, key : String) : void
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}
class QuantumComm {
    entangledPairs : List<DoubleArray2D>
    maxPairs : int
    random : Random
    jsonHelper : JsonHelper
    createEntangledPair() : DoubleArray2D
    formatMessage(recipient : String, message : String) : String
    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml

# Task
You will be given a **Requirement**, a **Class Diagram** in PlantUML, and a list of operation specifications. Your task is to correct the specifications that are inconsistent with the requirement.

An operation specification must describe the following sections about the operation:
1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;
5. Input: the input parameters, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.
2. For each operation specification, determine whether it is complete and consistent with the requirement.
3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EntanglementManager::establishConnection
Summary: Establishes a new active connection by creating a lower-level entangled pair. On success returns the 0-based index of the new active pair; if the lower-level pair capacity is exhausted, returns null instead of throwing.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to obtain a new entangled pair from quantumComm.createEntangledPair().
  2. If creation succeeds, add the returned DoubleArray2D object to activePairs.
  3. Return new EIntegerObject(activePairs.size() - 1).
  4. If createEntangledPair throws IllegalStateException because maxPairs has been reached, return null and do not modify activePairs.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D, EIntegerObject
Input: None.
Output: EIntegerObject containing the new active index, or null if the lower-level pair capacity has been reached.
Pre-condition: quantumComm has been initialized and its createEntangledPair operation is available.
Post-condition: If successful, a new pair exists in quantumComm.entangledPairs and at the end of activePairs; the returned index refers to that element. If capacity was reached, no state has changed and null is returned.

EntanglementManager::receiveMessage
Summary: Receives a message through an active connection. If the active index is invalid or communication fails, returns null; otherwise returns the human-readable received message.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return null.
  2. Let activePair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Find the lower-level index: lowerIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(activePair).
  4. If lowerIndex < 0, return null because the active pair has no backing lower-level pair.
  5. Call quantumComm.receiveMessage(lowerIndex).
  6. If quantumComm.receiveMessage returns null, return null; otherwise return the received String.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - the 0-based active-connection index.
Output: String - either "Message received with measurement: <0|1>" or null when the active index is invalid or a communication error occurs.
Pre-condition: activePairs and quantumComm are initialized.
Post-condition: System state is unchanged; invalid active indexes or communication failures produce null rather than exceptions.

EntanglementManager::sendMessage
Summary: Sends a message through an active connection. Invalid active indexes are ignored as no-ops; simulated transmission failure is tolerated and not reported because this method returns void.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return.
  2. Let activePair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Find lowerIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(activePair).
  4. If lowerIndex < 0, return.
  5. Call quantumComm.transmitMessage(message, lowerIndex). The returned boolean success/failure value is ignored; a failed transmission also results in no observable effect.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input: message : String - the message payload to transmit; pairIndex : int - 0-based active-connection index.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: activePairs and quantumComm are initialized; message is non-null.
Post-condition: If the active index is invalid, no action is taken. Otherwise the lower-level transmit operation is invoked; no exception is propagated from this method, and the active connection list is unchanged.

EntanglementManager::listActivePairs
Summary: Prints the currently active connections, including their indices and pair states, to standard output. The return type is void, so this operation is used for inspection/logging.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over activePairs with index i starting at 0.
  2. For each active pair, print i and the pair's 2x2 numeric values in a human-readable form.
  3. No return value is produced.
Related Features: activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: None.
Output: None; side effect is console output listing all active connections.
Pre-condition: activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition: activePairs is unchanged; a listing of active connections has been written to the output stream.

EntanglementManager::removePair
Summary: Removes an active connection from the active connection list. If the active index is invalid, the operation is a harmless no-op.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without changing anything.
  2. activePairs.remove(pairIndex).
Related Features: activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based active-connection index to remove.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition: If the index was valid, the active connection at that index is removed and later elements shift left. If the index was invalid, the active connection list remains unchanged. The lower-level quantumComm.entangledPairs pool is intentionally not changed.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Operation Specifications
EntanglementManager::establishConnection
Summary: Establishes a new active connection by creating a lower-level entangled pair. On success returns the 0-based index of the new active pair; if the lower-level pair capacity is exhausted, returns null instead of throwing.
Algorithm:
  1. Try to obtain a new entangled pair from quantumComm.createEntangledPair().
  2. If creation succeeds, add the returned DoubleArray2D object to activePairs.
  3. Return new EIntegerObject(activePairs.size() - 1).
  4. If createEntangledPair throws IllegalStateException because maxPairs has been reached, return null and do not modify activePairs.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D, EIntegerObject
Input: None.
Output: EIntegerObject containing the new active index, or null if the lower-level pair capacity has been reached.
Pre-condition: quantumComm has been initialized and its createEntangledPair operation is available.
Post-condition: If successful, a new pair exists in quantumComm.entangledPairs and at the end of activePairs; the returned index refers to that element. If capacity was reached, no state has changed and null is returned.

EntanglementManager::receiveMessage
Summary: Receives a message through an active connection. If the active index is invalid or communication fails, returns null; otherwise returns the human-readable received message.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return null.
  2. Let activePair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Find the lower-level index: lowerIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(activePair).
  4. If lowerIndex < 0, return null because the active pair has no backing lower-level pair.
  5. Call quantumComm.receiveMessage(lowerIndex).
  6. If quantumComm.receiveMessage returns null, return null; otherwise return the received String.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - the 0-based active-connection index.
Output: String - either "Message received with measurement: <0|1>" or null when the active index is invalid or a communication error occurs.
Pre-condition: activePairs and quantumComm are initialized.
Post-condition: System state is unchanged; invalid active indexes or communication failures produce null rather than exceptions.

EntanglementManager::sendMessage
Summary: Sends a message through an active connection. Invalid active indexes are ignored as no-ops; simulated transmission failure is tolerated and not reported because this method returns void.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return.
  2. Let activePair = activePairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Find lowerIndex = quantumComm.entangledPairs.indexOf(activePair).
  4. If lowerIndex < 0, return.
  5. Call quantumComm.transmitMessage(message, lowerIndex). The returned boolean success/failure value is ignored; a failed transmission also results in no observable effect.
Related Features: activePairs, quantumComm
Related Classes: QuantumComm, DoubleArray2D
Input: message : String - the message payload to transmit; pairIndex : int - 0-based active-connection index.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: activePairs and quantumComm are initialized; message is non-null.
Post-condition: If the active index is invalid, no action is taken. Otherwise the lower-level transmit operation is invoked; no exception is propagated from this method, and the active connection list is unchanged.

EntanglementManager::listActivePairs
Summary: Prints the currently active connections, including their indices and pair states, to standard output. The return type is void, so this operation is used for inspection/logging.
Algorithm:
  1. Iterate over activePairs with index i starting at 0.
  2. For each active pair, print i and the pair's 2x2 numeric values in a human-readable form.
  3. No return value is produced.
Related Features: activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: None.
Output: None; side effect is console output listing all active connections.
Pre-condition: activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition: activePairs is unchanged; a listing of active connections has been written to the output stream.

EntanglementManager::removePair
Summary: Removes an active connection from the active connection list. If the active index is invalid, the operation is a harmless no-op.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= activePairs.size(), return without changing anything.
  2. activePairs.remove(pairIndex).
Related Features: activePairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based active-connection index to remove.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: activePairs is initialized.
Post-condition: If the index was valid, the active connection at that index is removed and later elements shift left. If the index was invalid, the active connection list remains unchanged. The lower-level quantumComm.entangledPairs pool is intentionally not changed.

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary: Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true only for valid messages; invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException instead of returning false.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. For each required field in {"recipient", "message", "timestamp"}:
       if !messageDict.containsKey(field) or messageDict.get(field) == null or the value is not a String, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If messageDict contains "metadata" and its value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap - the message object to check.
Output: boolean - always true if the operation returns; invalid inputs are rejected by throwing.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: If valid, returns true and changes nothing. If invalid, throws IllegalArgumentException and changes nothing.

MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary: Formats a protocol message as a JSON string containing recipient, message, timestamp, and optional metadata.
Algorithm:
  1. If recipient == null or message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Create an EMap msg.
  3. Put "recipient" -> recipient, "message" -> message, and "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
  4. If metadata != null, put "metadata" -> metadata; otherwise omit it.
  5. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(msg).
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap (nullable).
Output: String - the serialized JSON object.
Pre-condition: recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned string is a valid JSON object string with the required basic fields and, if metadata was supplied, a "metadata" dictionary field.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary: Reads a metadata field from a message dictionary; returns null if the metadata dictionary or the requested key is absent.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata", or its value is not an EMap, return null.
  2. Let metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  3. Return metadataMap.get(key), which is null if the key is absent.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap, EJavaObject
Input: messageDict : EMap, key : String.
Output: EJavaObject - the stored metadata value, or null if missing.
Pre-condition: messageDict and key are non-null.
Post-condition: No state changes; the method only reads.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary: Adds or updates a metadata field inside a message dictionary, creating the metadata sub-dictionary if it does not already exist.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata" or its value is null, create a new empty EMap and put it under "metadata".
  2. If the existing "metadata" value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. Let metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  4. metadataMap.put(key, value).
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap, EJavaObject
Input: key : String - metadata field name; messageDict : EMap - message object; value : EJavaObject - value to store.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: messageDict is non-null; key is non-null.
Post-condition: messageDict has a "metadata" entry containing an EMap with key mapped to value. If metadata did not exist, it is created. If the existing metadata is not map-like, IllegalArgumentException is thrown and messageDict is unchanged.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary: Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary, rejecting null input, invalid JSON, and non-object input.
Algorithm:
  1. If rawMessage == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
  3. If result == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return result.
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output: EMap - parsed message object.
Pre-condition: None; invalid inputs are handled by exceptions.
Post-condition: If successful, returns an EMap corresponding to the input object. If rawMessage is null, is not valid JSON, or does not represent a JSON object, IllegalArgumentException is thrown.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary: Removes a metadata key from a message's metadata dictionary. If the metadata dictionary or the key is absent, the operation is harmless.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata", return.
  2. If the "metadata" value is not an EMap, return.
  3. metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  4. metadataMap.remove(key); if key is absent, no effect occurs.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap, key : String.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: messageDict is non-null.
Post-condition: If a metadata dictionary existed, the key is removed from it. A missing key or missing metadata dictionary leaves messageDict unchanged.

    measureState(pairIndex : int) : int
    parseMessage(rawMessage : String) : EMap
    receiveMessage(pairIndex : int) : String
    transmitMessage(message : String, pairIndex : int) : boolean
    validateMessage(messageDict : EMap) : boolean
}

EntanglementManager --> QuantumComm : quantumComm
MessageProtocol --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
QuantumComm --> JsonHelper : jsonHelper
@enduml
```

# Operation Specifications
MessageProtocol::validateMessage
Summary: Strictly validates a message dictionary. Returns true only for valid messages; invalid messages cause IllegalArgumentException instead of returning false.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. For each required field in {"recipient", "message", "timestamp"}:
       if !messageDict.containsKey(field) or messageDict.get(field) == null or the value is not a String, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If messageDict contains "metadata" and its value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap - the message object to check.
Output: boolean - always true if the operation returns; invalid inputs are rejected by throwing.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: If valid, returns true and changes nothing. If invalid, throws IllegalArgumentException and changes nothing.

MessageProtocol::formatMessage
Summary: Formats a protocol message as a JSON string containing recipient, message, timestamp, and optional metadata.
Algorithm:
  1. If recipient == null or message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Create an EMap msg.
  3. Put "recipient" -> recipient, "message" -> message, and "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
  4. If metadata != null, put "metadata" -> metadata; otherwise omit it.
  5. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(msg).
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: recipient : String, message : String, metadata : EMap (nullable).
Output: String - the serialized JSON object.
Pre-condition: recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned string is a valid JSON object string with the required basic fields and, if metadata was supplied, a "metadata" dictionary field.

MessageProtocol::getMetadata
Summary: Reads a metadata field from a message dictionary; returns null if the metadata dictionary or the requested key is absent.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata", or its value is not an EMap, return null.
  2. Let metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  3. Return metadataMap.get(key), which is null if the key is absent.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap, EJavaObject
Input: messageDict : EMap, key : String.
Output: EJavaObject - the stored metadata value, or null if missing.
Pre-condition: messageDict and key are non-null.
Post-condition: No state changes; the method only reads.

MessageProtocol::addMetadata
Summary: Adds or updates a metadata field inside a message dictionary, creating the metadata sub-dictionary if it does not already exist.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata" or its value is null, create a new empty EMap and put it under "metadata".
  2. If the existing "metadata" value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. Let metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  4. metadataMap.put(key, value).
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap, EJavaObject
Input: key : String - metadata field name; messageDict : EMap - message object; value : EJavaObject - value to store.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: messageDict is non-null; key is non-null.
Post-condition: messageDict has a "metadata" entry containing an EMap with key mapped to value. If metadata did not exist, it is created. If the existing metadata is not map-like, IllegalArgumentException is thrown and messageDict is unchanged.

MessageProtocol::parseMessage
Summary: Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary, rejecting null input, invalid JSON, and non-object input.
Algorithm:
  1. If rawMessage == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
  3. If result == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return result.
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output: EMap - parsed message object.
Pre-condition: None; invalid inputs are handled by exceptions.
Post-condition: If successful, returns an EMap corresponding to the input object. If rawMessage is null, is not valid JSON, or does not represent a JSON object, IllegalArgumentException is thrown.

MessageProtocol::removeMetadata
Summary: Removes a metadata key from a message's metadata dictionary. If the metadata dictionary or the key is absent, the operation is harmless.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict does not contain "metadata", return.
  2. If the "metadata" value is not an EMap, return.
  3. metadataMap = (EMap) messageDict.get("metadata").
  4. metadataMap.remove(key); if key is absent, no effect occurs.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap, key : String.
Output: None.
Pre-condition: messageDict is non-null.
Post-condition: If a metadata dictionary existed, the key is removed from it. A missing key or missing metadata dictionary leaves messageDict unchanged.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

# Operation Specifications
JsonHelper::skipWhitespace
Summary: Advances the parsing position in a JSON string past any whitespace characters.
Algorithm:
  1. Let i = pos[0].
  2. While i < s.length() and s.charAt(i) is ' ', '\t', '\n', or '\r', increment i.
  3. Set pos[0] = i.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: IntArrary
Input: s : String - the input JSON text; pos : IntArrary - an array whose first element holds the current parse index.
Output: None; pos[0] is updated to the index after the skipped whitespace.
Pre-condition: s is non-null; pos is non-null and has length at least 1; pos[0] is between 0 and s.length().
Post-condition: pos[0] points to the first non-whitespace character, or to s.length() if the remaining input is all whitespace. No other state is changed.

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary: Formats a basic message JSON string with recipient, message, and a generated ISO timestamp. This lower-level format does not include metadata.
Algorithm:
  1. If recipient == null or message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Build an EMap msg with "recipient" -> recipient, "message" -> message, and "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
  3. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(msg).
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: recipient : String, message : String.
Output: String - serialized JSON object.
Pre-condition: recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned string is a valid JSON object with recipient, message, and timestamp fields.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary: Strictly validates a message dictionary using the same rules as MessageProtocol. Returns true for valid messages and throws IllegalArgumentException for invalid ones.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Required fields "recipient", "message", and "timestamp" must be present, non-null, and String values; otherwise throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If "metadata" is present and its value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap - the message object to check.
Output: boolean - true if valid.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Valid messages return true; invalid messages throw IllegalArgumentException and change nothing.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary: Receives a message through a stored lower-level pair and returns a human-readable string. A simulated communication error may return null.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If random.nextDouble() < COMMUNICATION_FAILURE_PROBABILITY, a small inferred probability such as 0.05, return null.
  3. int value = measureState(pairIndex).
  4. Return "Message received with measurement: " + value.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random, measureState
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: String - "Message received with measurement: <0|1>", or null if a simulated communication error occurs.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. If communication fails, null is returned; otherwise the human-readable message is returned.

QuantumComm::measureState
Summary: Measures the entangled pair at the given lower-level pool index and returns a binary state 0 or 1.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. DoubleArray2D pair = entangledPairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Compute total = pair.get(0,0) + pair.get(0,1) + pair.get(1,0) + pair.get(1,1).
  4. Return total >= 2.0 ? 1 : 0.
Related Features: entangledPairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based index into the stored lower-level pair pool.
Output: int - either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes; measurement is deterministic for the same pair. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary: Transmits a message through a stored lower-level pair. Returns true on successful transmission and false when a simulated random failure occurs.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If random.nextDouble() < COMMUNICATION_FAILURE_PROBABILITY, a small inferred probability such as 0.05, return false.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: message : String - the message payload; pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: boolean - true if the transmission succeeds; false if the simulated channel failure occurs.
Pre-condition: message is non-null; pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range.
Post-condition: No pair state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. The returned boolean reports whether the simulated transmission succeeded.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary: Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary, rejecting null input, invalid JSON, and non-object input.
Algorithm:
  1. If rawMessage == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
  3. If result == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return result.
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output: EMap - parsed message object.
Pre-condition: None; invalid inputs are handled by exceptions.
Post-condition: If successful, returns an EMap corresponding to the input object. If rawMessage is null, is not valid JSON, or does not represent a JSON object, IllegalArgumentException is thrown.

1. Summary: the summary of functionality;
2. Algorithm: algorithm or pseudo code;
3. Related Features: a list of structural or behavior features within this class that shall be accessed;
4. Related Classes: a list of classes and data types defined in **Class Diagram** that shall be accessed, in the format of `Related Classes: ClsA, CLsB, ...` or `Related Classes: None`;

6. Output: output value, including the meaning, the format, and the value ranges if necessary;
7. Pre-condition: the condition that the system and the input must satisfy before the operation is executed;
8. Post-condition: the condition that the system and the output must satisfy after the operation is executed.

Finish this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Read the requirement and the class diagram.

3. If there is any error, correct the erroneous specification.
4. Follow the specification format. Output changed specifications only; OMIT unchanged specifications.

# Requirement
```text
# Requirement: KOSASIH_celestrion-nexus-core

## 1. Overall / Domain Description

This system simulates a small quantum communication layer. It manages entangled-pair resources, performs simplified measurements, sends and receives messages through those pairs, and represents transmitted messages as JSON-like dictionaries. The model is not a physics engine; it provides deterministic application-level behavior around a randomized communication primitive.

## 2. Functional Requirement

  An entangled pair is represented as a two-by-two numeric state generated from random amplitudes. The communication layer has a maximum number of stored pairs. Creating a pair adds it to the available
  pool until that limit is reached; attempts beyond the limit must throw `IllegalStateException`.

  Measurement uses a pair index and produces a binary result, either `0` or `1`. Operations that directly address the stored pair pool must reject indexes outside the available range by throwing
  `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. This applies to measuring a pair and transmitting through a stored pair. Message transmission should succeed most of the time but include a small simulated random failure
  probability.

  Receiving from a valid pair should produce a human-readable message in the form `Message received with measurement: <value>`, where `<value>` is the measured binary state.

  Message payloads are represented as JSON objects. A basic message contains `recipient`, `message`, and `timestamp`. Timestamps should be generated at formatting time using an ISO instant style.
  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.
```

    random : Random

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary: Formats a basic message JSON string with recipient, message, and a generated ISO timestamp. This lower-level format does not include metadata.
Algorithm:
  1. If recipient == null or message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Build an EMap msg with "recipient" -> recipient, "message" -> message, and "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
  3. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(msg).
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: recipient : String, message : String.
Output: String - serialized JSON object.
Pre-condition: recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned string is a valid JSON object with recipient, message, and timestamp fields.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary: Strictly validates a message dictionary using the same rules as MessageProtocol. Returns true for valid messages and throws IllegalArgumentException for invalid ones.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Required fields "recipient", "message", and "timestamp" must be present, non-null, and String values; otherwise throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If "metadata" is present and its value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap - the message object to check.
Output: boolean - true if valid.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Valid messages return true; invalid messages throw IllegalArgumentException and change nothing.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary: Receives a message through a stored lower-level pair and returns a human-readable string. A simulated communication error may return null.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If random.nextDouble() < COMMUNICATION_FAILURE_PROBABILITY, a small inferred probability such as 0.05, return null.
  3. int value = measureState(pairIndex).
  4. Return "Message received with measurement: " + value.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random, measureState
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: String - "Message received with measurement: <0|1>", or null if a simulated communication error occurs.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. If communication fails, null is returned; otherwise the human-readable message is returned.

QuantumComm::measureState
Summary: Measures the entangled pair at the given lower-level pool index and returns a binary state 0 or 1.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. DoubleArray2D pair = entangledPairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Compute total = pair.get(0,0) + pair.get(0,1) + pair.get(1,0) + pair.get(1,1).
  4. Return total >= 2.0 ? 1 : 0.
Related Features: entangledPairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based index into the stored lower-level pair pool.
Output: int - either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes; measurement is deterministic for the same pair. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary: Transmits a message through a stored lower-level pair. Returns true on successful transmission and false when a simulated random failure occurs.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If random.nextDouble() < COMMUNICATION_FAILURE_PROBABILITY, a small inferred probability such as 0.05, return false.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: message : String - the message payload; pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: boolean - true if the transmission succeeds; false if the simulated channel failure occurs.
Pre-condition: message is non-null; pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range.
Post-condition: No pair state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. The returned boolean reports whether the simulated transmission succeeded.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary: Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary, rejecting null input, invalid JSON, and non-object input.
Algorithm:
  1. If rawMessage == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
  3. If result == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return result.
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output: EMap - parsed message object.
Pre-condition: None; invalid inputs are handled by exceptions.
Post-condition: If successful, returns an EMap corresponding to the input object. If rawMessage is null, is not valid JSON, or does not represent a JSON object, IllegalArgumentException is thrown.

  Protocol-level messages may also include `metadata`, which should be a dictionary when present.

  Parsing must reject null input, invalid JSON, or non-object input by throwing `IllegalArgumentException`. Message validation is strict: `validateMessage` returns `true` for valid messages, and must
  throw `IllegalArgumentException` for invalid messages rather than returning `false`. A message is invalid if it is null, lacks `recipient`, `message`, or `timestamp`, has a null required field, or
  contains `metadata` whose value is not map-like. Metadata updates should create the metadata dictionary when needed, removing a missing key should be harmless, and reading missing metadata should
  return no value.

  The higher-level entanglement manager tracks active connections separately from the lower-level communication pool. Establishing a connection returns the active-pair index on success and no value if
  the lower-level pair capacity has been reached. The manager acts as a tolerant facade: sending through an invalid active index should be a no-op, receiving through an invalid active index or through
  a communication error should return no message, and removing an invalid active index should leave the active connection list unchanged.

## 3. External / Tool Classes

There is no separate helper class required outside the UML model.

# Operation Specifications
QuantumComm::formatMessage
Summary: Formats a basic message JSON string with recipient, message, and a generated ISO timestamp. This lower-level format does not include metadata.
Algorithm:
  1. If recipient == null or message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Build an EMap msg with "recipient" -> recipient, "message" -> message, and "timestamp" -> Instant.now().toString().
  3. Return jsonHelper.serializeMap(msg).
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: recipient : String, message : String.
Output: String - serialized JSON object.
Pre-condition: recipient and message are non-null.
Post-condition: The returned string is a valid JSON object with recipient, message, and timestamp fields.

QuantumComm::validateMessage
Summary: Strictly validates a message dictionary using the same rules as MessageProtocol. Returns true for valid messages and throws IllegalArgumentException for invalid ones.
Algorithm:
  1. If messageDict == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. Required fields "recipient", "message", and "timestamp" must be present, non-null, and String values; otherwise throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If "metadata" is present and its value is not an EMap, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: None
Related Classes: EMap
Input: messageDict : EMap - the message object to check.
Output: boolean - true if valid.
Pre-condition: None.
Post-condition: Valid messages return true; invalid messages throw IllegalArgumentException and change nothing.

QuantumComm::receiveMessage
Summary: Receives a message through a stored lower-level pair and returns a human-readable string. A simulated communication error may return null.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If random.nextDouble() < 0.05, return null.
  3. int value = measureState(pairIndex).
  4. Return "Message received with measurement: " + value.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random, measureState
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: String - "Message received with measurement: <0|1>", or null if a simulated communication error occurs.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. If communication fails, null is returned; otherwise the human-readable message is returned.

QuantumComm::measureState
Summary: Measures the entangled pair at the given lower-level pool index and returns a binary state 0 or 1.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. DoubleArray2D pair = entangledPairs.get(pairIndex).
  3. Compute total = pair.get(0,0) + pair.get(0,1) + pair.get(1,0) + pair.get(1,1).
  4. Return total >= 2.0 ? 1 : 0.
Related Features: entangledPairs
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: pairIndex : int - 0-based index into the stored lower-level pair pool.
Output: int - either 0 or 1.
Pre-condition: pairIndex is within the available pair range; otherwise IndexOutOfBoundsException is thrown.
Post-condition: No state changes; measurement is deterministic for the same pair. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.

QuantumComm::transmitMessage
Summary: Transmits a message through a stored lower-level pair. Returns true on successful transmission and false when a simulated random failure occurs.
Algorithm:
  1. If pairIndex < 0 or pairIndex >= entangledPairs.size(), throw IndexOutOfBoundsException.
  2. If message == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  3. If random.nextDouble() < 0.05, return false.
  4. Return true.
Related Features: entangledPairs, random
Related Classes: DoubleArray2D
Input: message : String - the message payload; pairIndex : int - 0-based lower-level pair index.
Output: boolean - true if the transmission succeeds; false if the simulated channel failure occurs.
Pre-condition: message is non-null; pairIndex is within the available lower-level pair range.
Post-condition: No pair state changes. Invalid indexes throw IndexOutOfBoundsException. The returned boolean reports whether the simulated transmission succeeded.

QuantumComm::parseMessage
Summary: Parses a raw JSON string into a message dictionary, rejecting null input, invalid JSON, and non-object input.
Algorithm:
  1. If rawMessage == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  2. EMap result = jsonHelper.parseObject(rawMessage).
  3. If result == null, throw IllegalArgumentException.
  4. Return result.
Related Features: jsonHelper
Related Classes: JsonHelper, EMap
Input: rawMessage : String - JSON text.
Output: EMap - parsed message object.
Pre-condition: None; invalid inputs are handled by exceptions.
Post-condition: If successful, returns an EMap corresponding to the input object. If rawMessage is null, is not valid JSON, or does not represent a JSON object, IllegalArgumentException is thrown.

  3.3 You should not change the method signature;

  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

You will be given a **Java Class** generated from an Ecore model by EMF code generator with `suppressInterfaces=true`. Your task is to implement the Java methods unimplemented by taking additional contextual information into account.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. A method is unimplemented if its body contains `// TODO: Please complete this Java method based on the docstring`.
3. For every unimplemented method, 
  3.1 You must read the docstring of this method. The fragment between `<!-- begin-model-doc -->` and `<!-- end-model-doc -->` contains the method specification;
  3.2 Generate your code strictly according to the algorithm; Be aware of the EMF code style and APIs;
  3.3 You should not change the method signature;
  3.4 After generation, replace the current body with the one you generate;
  3.5 Keep and update the docstring by changing `@generated` to `@generated NOT`.
4. Check the import list. Add missing imports if needed.
5. Do not change other part.
6. Be aware of the method signatures, particularly, the input and output types, provided in the context.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.

- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.

Complete this task by following the guidelines below.
1. Compute context information
  1.1 Consider the related classes in the Javadocs.
  1.2 Locate these files in your project.
  1.3 Generate the class summarization.
2. Read the error messages carefully.

5. Do not change parts without errors.

## EMF Coding Style
- When possible, call getter/setter methods to access field values. Do not read/write raw fields derived from the class diagram.
- The getter of a boolean attribute `bv` looks like `isBv()`, e.g., `isIsTrue()` for `isTrue`.
- When possibe, use the creation factory for object creation.
- To create an EList instance, import and use `org.eclipse.emf.common.util.BasicEList`.
